<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Solomon–Yau least-volume conjecture for minimal hypersurfaces of unit round spheres: in every dimension <i>m</i> ≥ 2, a closed connected minimal immersion with non-totally-geodesic image has volume at least the smallest <i>m</i>-dimensional minimal Clifford product. Volume is measured on the domain, so covering multiplicities are included.

- **claim-volume-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-volume)): Let $m\ge2$, let $M^m$ be a closed connected smooth manifold, and let $F:M\to S^{m+1}$ be a smooth minimal immersion into the unit round sphere. If $F(M)$ is not totally geodesic, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}\bigl(M,F^*g_{\mathrm{round}}\bigr)\ge a_m.$$
  - Explanation: Every closed connected minimal immersion into a unit round sphere whose image is not totally geodesic has domain volume at least the smallest minimal Clifford product volume. Covering multiplicities are counted. This resolves the least-volume conjecture in every dimension from two upward.
- **claim-clifford-ratio-decrease** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-clifford-ratios)): The numbers $\vartheta_m=a_m/\sigma_m$ strictly decrease for integers $m\ge2$. Moreover, $$\vartheta_2=\frac{\pi}{2}<2, \qquad \vartheta_4\le\frac32.$$ Consequently $a_m<2\sigma_m$ for every $m\ge2$, and $\vartheta_m\le3/2$ for every $m\ge4$.
  - Explanation: The ratio of the smallest Clifford volume to the equator volume strictly decreases with dimension. Explicit values in the first even dimensions give the uniform bound below twice the equator volume and the sharper bound for higher dimensions.
- **claim-immersion-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-immersion-reduction)): Let $F:M^m\to S^{m+1}$ be a smooth minimal immersion of a closed manifold, with $m\ge2$. If some image point has $q$ preimages, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}\bigl(M,F^*g_{\mathrm{round}}\bigr)\ge q\sigma_m.$$ In particular, every immersion violating the bound in Theorem <a href="#thm:volume" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:volume">1</a> is an embedding.
  - Explanation: A point with several preimages forces the domain volume to be at least that many equator volumes, by monotonicity of the cone over the immersion. Hence any immersion violating the volume bound must be an embedding.
- **claim-fullness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fullness)): A closed connected embedded non-equatorial minimal hypersurface $\Sigma^m\subset S^{m+1}$ is two-sided and full, meaning that its position vectors span $\mathbb R^{m+2}$. It is real analytic, and $$\int_\Sigma x=0.$$ For every nonzero constant vector $a$, the coordinate function $\langle a,x\rangle$ is nontrivial and its zero set has $m$-dimensional measure zero.
  - Explanation: A non-equatorial closed embedded minimal hypersurface in a sphere is two-sided, spans the whole ambient Euclidean space, is analytic, and has vanishing integral of the position vector. Linear coordinate functions have null zero sets.
- **claim-index-upper-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-upper-tests)): The function $\mu$ in <a href="#eq:index-mu" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:index-mu">[eq:index-mu]</a> is finite, continuous, and strictly positive. If a smooth positive function $\phi$ satisfies $\phi\ge\mu$ in a neighborhood of $x$ and $\phi(x)=\mu(x)$, then $$\label{eq:index-log-test} \Delta\log\phi(x)\ge m-P(x).$$
  - Explanation: The two-sided curvature maximum function is finite, continuous and positive, and any smooth upper test touching it satisfies a logarithmic differential inequality involving the squared second fundamental form.
- **claim-curvature-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-curvature-average)): Every smooth, closed, connected, embedded minimal hypersurface $\Sigma^m\subset S^{m+1}$, $m\ge2$, which is not totally geodesic satisfies $$\label{eq:index-curvature-average} \int_\Sigma \|A\|^2\,dV\ge m\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_m(\Sigma).$$
  - Explanation: For a non-totally-geodesic closed embedded minimal hypersurface of the sphere, the integral of the squared second fundamental form is at least the dimension times the volume. The proof compares the curvature maximum with a Poisson potential.
- **claim-normal-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-normal-independence)): The map $a\mapsto\langle a,N\rangle$ from $\mathbb R^{m+2}$ to $C^\infty(\Sigma)$ is injective.
  - Explanation: The normal coordinate functions attached to distinct ambient vectors are linearly independent, because a vanishing one would force the hypersurface to be totally geodesic by sphere rigidity.
- **claim-conformal-balancing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-conformal-tests)): For every smooth positive weight $u$ on $\Sigma$, there is a map $T_{\tau,a}$ in <a href="#eq:index-conformal-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:index-conformal-map">[eq:index-conformal-map]</a> such that $$\label{eq:index-balance} \int_\Sigma u\,T_{\tau,a}(x)\,dV=0.$$ For every $a\in S^{m+1}$ and $\tau\ge0$, $$\label{eq:index-conformal-area} \int_\Sigma\beta_{\tau,a}^{\,m}\,dV\le V_\Sigma,$$ with strict inequality if $\tau>0$. Consequently, $$\label{eq:index-conformal-energy} \int_\Sigma\beta_{\tau,a}^{\,2}\,dV\le V_\Sigma,$$ again with strict inequality if $\tau>0$.
  - Explanation: For any positive weight there is a conformal transformation of the sphere balancing the weighted center of mass. Non-trivial transformations strictly decrease the integrals of the conformal factor powers.
- **claim-index-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-index-rigidity)): Every smooth, closed, connected, embedded minimal hypersurface $\Sigma^m\subset S^{m+1}$, $m\ge2$, which is not totally geodesic has $$\mathop{\mathrm{ind}}(\Sigma)\ge m+3.$$ If $\mathop{\mathrm{ind}}(\Sigma)=m+3$, then, up to an ambient isometry, its image is $$S^k\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\right) \times S^{m-k}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{m-k}{m}}\right) \quad\text{for some }1\le k<m.$$ In particular, such a hypersurface of volume less than $a_m$ has index strictly greater than $m+3$.
  - Explanation: The Morse index of a non-totally-geodesic closed embedded minimal hypersurface of the sphere is at least dimension plus three, and equality forces a minimal Clifford product. Hence smaller-volume examples have strictly larger index.
- **claim-filling-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-minmax-cover)): The boundary map $$\pi:\mathcal C(S^{m+1})\longrightarrow \mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\mathbb Z_2),\qquad \pi(E)=\partial[E],$$ is a two-sheeted covering map for the indicated topologies. Its deck involution is $E\mapsto E^c=S^{m+1}\setminus E$.
  - Explanation: Taking the mod-two boundary of a finite-perimeter set gives a two-sheeted covering onto the zero component of cycles, with complementation as deck transformation.
- **claim-projective-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-minmax-projective)): Set $Q=\overline B^{m+2}\times[-1,1]$. Suppose $q\mapsto E_q\in\mathcal C(S^{m+1})$ is continuous in volume, with $$E_{-q}=E_q^c\quad(q\in\partial Q),\qquad E_{(0,-1)}=\varnothing,\quad E_{(0,1)}=S^{m+1}.$$ Then $q\mapsto\partial[E_q]$ descends to a detecting map on $Q/(q\sim-q\text{ on }\partial Q)\cong\mathbb{RP}^{p}$.
  - Explanation: A family of finite-perimeter sets over a ball-times-interval, continuous in volume and complementary at antipodal boundary points, descends to a detecting map on real projective space.
- **bound-detection-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-detection-gap)): There is a constant $\gamma_m>\sigma_m$, depending only on $m$, such that every detecting map satisfies $$\sup_{x\in X}\mathbf M(\Phi(x))\ge\gamma_m.$$
  - Explanation: Every detecting map has a cycle whose mass is at least a dimensional constant strictly above the equator volume. The constant comes from a centroid-constrained half-volume isoperimetric problem.
- **claim-cubical-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-minmax-cubical)): A detecting map on $\mathbb{RP}^{p}$ can be pulled back to a detecting map on a finite cubical complex, preserving its mass supremum and its no-concentration property.
  - Explanation: A detecting map on projective space can be pulled back to a finite cubical complex through a retraction, keeping the mass supremum and the absence of concentration.
- **claim-minmax-consequence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-minmax)): Let $m\ge2$, and let $\Phi:X\to\mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\mathbb Z_2)$ be a detecting map on a finite cubical complex, with no concentration of mass and $$D_0=\sup_{x\in X}\mathbf M(\Phi(x))<\infty.$$ There is a stationary integral $m$-varifold $V$ in $S^{m+1}$ such that $$\label{eq:minmax-output} \gamma_m\le\\|V\\|(S^{m+1})\le D_0.$$ If $2\le m\le6$, $V$ can be chosen as a finite sum of smooth closed embedded minimal hypersurfaces with positive integer multiplicities. The same conclusions hold for a family on $\mathbb{RP}^{p}$.
  - Explanation: A detecting family without concentration yields a stationary integral varifold whose mass lies between the detection gap and the maximal mass of the family, smooth embedded in low dimensions.
- **claim-lorentz-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-lorentz-family)): Let $p=m+3$ and $$Q=\overline B^{m+2}\times[-1,1],\qquad u=(v,b).$$ Identify opposite points of $\partial Q$, and denote the quotient map by $q:Q\longrightarrow\mathbb {RP}^{p}$. There is a flat-continuous map $$\Phi:\mathbb {RP}^{p}\longrightarrow \mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\mathbb Z_2)$$ with the following properties.* 1. *The map $\Phi$ is detecting in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:detecting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:detecting">12</a>, has no concentration of mass, and satisfies $$\Phi(q(0,0))=\partial[\Omega],\qquad \mathbf M(\Phi(q(0,0)))=\|\Sigma\|,$$ where $\Omega$ is the side of $\Sigma$ opposite to $N$. For $r=\sqrt{1-b^2}$ its masses obey $$\label{eq:family-mass-bounds} \mathbf M(\Phi(q(v,b)))\le \begin{cases} r^m\|\Sigma\|,& \|v\|<1,\\ r^m\sigma_m,& \|v\|=1. \end{cases}$$ In particular $\|\Sigma\|\ge\gamma_m>\sigma_m$, where $\gamma_m$ is the constant in Lemma <a href="#lem:detection-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:detection-gap">14</a>.* 2. *The central parameter is the unique maximum, in the uniform sense that for every neighborhood $U$ of $(0,0)$ in $Q$, $$\label{eq:family-away-gap} \sup_{u\in Q\setminus U}\mathbf M(\Phi(q(u)))<\|\Sigma\|.$$* 3. *Near $(0,0)$ these cycles are precisely the multiplicity-one boundaries of a smooth family of domains, with embedded boundaries parametrized by $\Sigma$. The normal derivative at the center, in the parameter direction $(d,t)\in\mathbb R^{m+2}\times\mathbb R$, is $$\label{eq:family-normal-variations} w_{d,t}(x)=t+\langle d,x\rangle.$$ These variations form a $p$-dimensional subspace on which the area second variation is negative definite.* 4. *If $\mathop{\mathrm{ind}}(\Sigma)>p$, there is a flat homotopy of $\Phi$, supported in the image of a neighborhood of $(0,0)$, to a detecting map $\widetilde\Phi$ with no concentration of mass and $$\label{eq:family-strict-deformation} \sup_{\xi\in\mathbb {RP}^{p}}\mathbf M(\widetilde\Phi(\xi))<\|\Sigma\|.$$
  - Explanation: Boosting the graph of signed cone distance produces a detecting projective family of cycles with no concentration, maximal mass exactly at the central parameter, and a local deformation lowering that maximum when the index is large.
- **claim-cone-contact** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-cone-contact)): There is $K<1$ such that $$\label{eq:family-spacelike-bound} \|\varphi(z)\|\le K\|z\|\qquad(z\in\mathbb R^{m+2}).$$ For $z\ne0$, put $h=\varphi(z)$. Every closest point of $\mathcal C$ to $z$ has the form $r_zx$, with $x\in\Sigma$ and $$\label{eq:family-contact} r_z=\sqrt{\|z\|^2-h^2}>0,\qquad z=r_zx+hN(x),\qquad r_z I-hA(x)\ge0.$$ The final inequality is an inequality of self-adjoint endomorphisms of $T_x\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: Signed distance to the cone is bounded by a constant below one times the norm, and every closest cone point has a described form with a nonnegativity condition on the shape operator.
- **claim-global-graph** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-graph)): For each $v\in B^{m+2}$ the inverse image under <a href="#eq:family-boost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:family-boost">[eq:family-boost]</a> of $h=\varphi(z)$ is the graph of a continuous function $b=\psi_v(y)$ on all of $\mathbb R^{m+2}$. The functions depend continuously on $(v,y)$. On $S^{m+1}$ they satisfy $\|\psi_v\|<1$ and extend jointly continuously to $\overline B^{m+2}\times S^{m+1}$ by $$\label{eq:family-boundary-graph} \psi_a(y)=-\langle a,y\rangle\qquad(\|a\|=1).$$
  - Explanation: After a Lorentz boost, the distance graph remains a graph of a continuous function over all of Euclidean space, with a continuous compactified extension to infinite boosts.
- **claim-all-levels-perimeter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-all-levels)): Every $E_{v,b}$, for $(v,b)\in Q$, has finite perimeter. For $\|v\|<1$, $\|b\|<1$ and every open set $U\subset S^{m+1}$, $$\label{eq:family-restricted-perimeter} \begin{split} \operatorname{Per}(E_{v,b};U) &\le\mathcal H^m(\Lambda_{v,b}\cap U)\\ &\le\int_{T_{v,b}\cap Y_{v,b}^{-1}(U)} \det(rI-h_{v,b}A)\,d\mu, \end{split}$$ and on $T_{v,b}$, $$\label{eq:family-restricted-density} 0\le\det(rI-h_{v,b}A)\le r^m\le1.$$ Thus these estimates hold at every level, without an exceptional set of values of $b$.
  - Explanation: Every sublevel set in the family has finite perimeter, bounded by an integral of a restricted determinant over a source set, with the determinant at most a power of the level radius.
- **claim-no-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-no-concentration)): The family $\widehat\Phi$ satisfies $$\label{eq:family-no-concentration} \lim_{\rho\downarrow0}\; \sup_{(v,b)\in Q}\sup_{y\in S^{m+1}} \\|\widehat\Phi(v,b)\\|(B_\rho(y))=0.$$
  - Explanation: Masses of the family cycles in small balls tend to zero uniformly over parameters, including infinite boosts, by dominated convergence on the fixed source hypersurface.
- **claim-strict-loss** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-strict-loss)): For every neighborhood $U$ of $(0,0)$, <a href="#eq:family-away-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:family-away-gap">[eq:family-away-gap]</a> holds.
  - Explanation: Away from any neighborhood of the central parameter the mass stays uniformly below the area of the original hypersurface, using a fixed source patch where the second fundamental form is nonzero.
- **claim-central-tube** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-central-tube)): For all sufficiently small $(v,b)$, the entire level $\Lambda_{v,b}$ is $Y_{v,b}(\Sigma)$, with $Y_{v,b}$ a smooth embedding depending smoothly on the parameters. Every level point has a unique closest-point contact in the cone representation. Moreover $$\label{eq:family-regular-level} \nabla_{S^{m+1}}\psi_v=N'-bY_{v,b},\qquad \|\nabla_{S^{m+1}}\psi_v\|=r>0 \quad\hbox{on }\Lambda_{v,b}.$$ Thus $\Lambda_{v,b}=\partial E_{v,b}=\partial^*E_{v,b}$ and its boundary cycle has multiplicity one.
  - Explanation: Near the center, the whole level is a smooth embedded copy of the hypersurface with unique closest-point contacts and a multiplicity-one boundary cycle.
- **claim-cone-operation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cone-operation)): Let $d\geq1$ and let $V$ be a stationary integral $d$-varifold in the unit sphere $S^{d+1}$. Its Euclidean cone $\mathcal C(V)$ is a stationary integral $(d+1)$-varifold in $\mathbb R^{d+2}$, and $$\label{eq:cones-mass} \\|\mathcal C(V)\\|(B_R(0)) =\frac{R^{d+1}}{d+1}\mathbf M(V), \qquad \Theta(\mathcal C(V),0)=\frac{\mathbf M(V)}{\sigma_d}.$$ If $V$ is a finite sum of smooth closed embedded minimal hypersurfaces with positive integer multiplicities, then $\mathcal C(V)$ is cyclic modulo two.
  - Explanation: The Euclidean cone over a stationary integral spherical varifold is stationary and integral, with explicit ball masses and vertex density, and is cyclic modulo two for smooth multiplicity-weighted hypersurface sums.
- **claim-cones-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cones-density)): Let $V$ be a stationary integral $k$-varifold in an open set $U\subset\mathbb R^n$. At every $z\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\\|V\\|$, $\Theta(V,z)\geq1$. If $V=\mathcal C$ is a cone in all of $\mathbb R^n$, then $$\label{eq:cones-density-comparison} 1\leq\Theta(\mathcal C,z)\leq\Theta(\mathcal C,0) \qquad (z\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\\|\mathcal C\\|).$$ Every tangent cone at such a point is nonzero and has density $\Theta(\mathcal C,z)$ at its vertex.
  - Explanation: Stationary integral varifolds have density at least one at every support point; for cones the vertex density bounds the density elsewhere, and tangent cones inherit the density.
- **claim-cones-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cones-splitting)): Let $k\geq2$ and let $\mathcal C$ be a stationary integral $k$-dimensional cone in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$. For $0\ne z\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\\|\mathcal C\\|$, every tangent cone $T$ at $z$ has the form $$T=\mathbf v(\mathbb Re)\times W,\qquad e=z/\|z\|,$$ where $W$ is a nonzero stationary integral $(k-1)$-dimensional cone in $e^\perp\cong\mathbb R^k$ and $$\label{eq:cones-factor-density} \Theta(W,0)=\Theta(T,0)=\Theta(\mathcal C,z).$$ If $\mathcal C$ is cyclic modulo two, so are $T$ and $W$.
  - Explanation: Tangent cones of a stationary integral cone at nonzero support points split off a line, leaving a lower-dimensional stationary integral cone with the same density and preserved cyclicity.
- **claim-cones-allard** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cones-allard)): Let $V$ be a stationary integral $k$-varifold, $k\geq2$, in an open subset of Euclidean space. If one tangent cone at $z$ is a multiplicity-one plane, then in a neighborhood of $z$, $V$ is a smooth embedded minimal submanifold with multiplicity one.
  - Explanation: If one tangent cone of a stationary integral varifold is a multiplicity-one plane, the varifold is a smooth embedded minimal submanifold with multiplicity one near that point, by interior regularity.
- **claim-low-density-cones** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-low-density-cones)): Fix $m\geq2$ and assume Theorem <a href="#thm:volume" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:volume">1</a> in dimensions $2,\ldots,m-1$, with an empty assumption when $m=2$. Let $\mathcal C$ be a nonzero stationary integral $k$-dimensional cone in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$ such that $$\label{eq:cones-threshold} \Theta(\mathcal C,0)<\vartheta_m.$$ When $m=2$ or $m=3$, also assume that $\mathcal C$ is cyclic modulo two. Then:* 1. *if $1\leq k\leq m$, $\mathcal C$ is a multiplicity-one hyperplane;* 2. *if $k=m+1$, $\mathcal C$ is smooth embedded with multiplicity one away from its vertex. Its link in $S^{m+1}$ is a smooth closed connected embedded minimal hypersurface.
  - Explanation: Given the volume theorem in lower dimensions, stationary integral cones with vertex density below the Clifford ratio are hyperplanes up to dimension m, and in dimension m plus one are smooth with a smooth connected minimal link.
- **claim-spherical-output** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-cones-spherical-output)): Under the outer induction hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#prop:low-density-cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:low-density-cones">29</a>, let $V\ne0$ be a stationary integral $m$-varifold in $S^{m+1}$ with $\mathbf M(V)<a_m$. If $m=2$ or $3$, assume additionally that $\mathcal C(V)$ is cyclic modulo two; this holds, in particular, if $V$ has smooth closed embedded support with integer component multiplicities. Then $V=\mathbf v(\Sigma)$ for a smooth closed connected embedded minimal hypersurface $\Sigma$. If $\mathbf M(V)>\sigma_m$, this hypersurface is not an equator.
  - Explanation: Under the induction hypothesis, a nonzero stationary integral spherical varifold of mass below the Clifford threshold is a smooth connected embedded multiplicity-one minimal hypersurface, non-equatorial if its mass exceeds the equator volume.
- **claim-least-counterexample** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-attainment)): Fix $m\geq2$ and assume Theorem <a href="#thm:volume" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:volume">1</a> in dimensions $2,\ldots,m-1$. Suppose the class $\mathcal E_m$ of smooth closed connected embedded minimal hypersurfaces $\Sigma\subset S^{m+1}$ satisfying $$\Sigma\text{ is not totally geodesic},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(\Sigma)<a_m$$ is nonempty. Then $$H=\inf_{\Sigma\in\mathcal E_m}\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(\Sigma)$$ is attained by some $\Sigma_*\in\mathcal E_m$, and $$\label{eq:cones-attainment-gap} \sigma_m<\gamma_m\leq H<a_m.$$ Here $\gamma_m$ is the detection gap from Lemma <a href="#lem:detection-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:detection-gap">14</a>.
  - Explanation: If sub-threshold non-totally-geodesic embedded minimal hypersurfaces exist, the infimum of their areas is attained by one of them and lies strictly between the detection gap and the Clifford threshold.

          "exact_quote": "For every smooth positive weight $u$ on $\\Sigma$, there is a map $T_{\\tau,a}$ in <a href=\"#eq:index-conformal-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:index-conformal-map\">[eq:index-conformal-map]</a> such that $$\\label{eq:index-balance}\n \\int_\\Sigma u\\,T_{\\tau,a}(x)\\,dV=0.$$ For every $a\\in S^{m+1}$ and $\\tau\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:index-conformal-area}\n \\int_\\Sigma\\beta_{\\tau,a}^{\\,m}\\,dV\\le V_\\Sigma,$$ with strict inequality if $\\tau>0$. Consequently, $$\\label{eq:index-conformal-energy}\n \\int_\\Sigma\\beta_{\\tau,a}^{\\,2}\\,dV\\le V_\\Sigma,$$ again with strict inequality if $\\tau>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-349.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every smooth positive weight $u$ on $\\Sigma$, there is a map $T_{\\tau,a}$ in <a href=\"#eq:index-conformal-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:index-conformal-map\">[eq:index-conformal-map]</a> such that $$\\label{eq:index-balance}\n \\int_\\Sigma u\\,T_{\\tau,a}(x)\\,dV=0.$$ For every $a\\in S^{m+1}$ and $\\tau\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:index-conformal-area}\n \\int_\\Sigma\\beta_{\\tau,a}^{\\,m}\\,dV\\le V_\\Sigma,$$ with strict inequality if $\\tau>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-index-rigidity",
        "exact_quote": "Every smooth, closed, connected, embedded minimal hypersurface $\\Sigma^m\\subset S^{m+1}$, $m\\ge2$, which is not totally geodesic has $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)\\ge m+3.$$ If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)=m+3$, then, up to an ambient isometry, its image is $$S^k\\!\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{k}{m}}\\right)\n \\times\n S^{m-k}\\!\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{m-k}{m}}\\right)\n \\quad\\text{for some }1\\le k<m.$$ In particular, such a hypersurface of volume less than $a_m$ has index strictly greater than $m+3$.",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-index-rigidity",
          "exact_quote": "Every smooth, closed, connected, embedded minimal hypersurface $\\Sigma^m\\subset S^{m+1}$, $m\\ge2$, which is not totally geodesic has $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)\\ge m+3.$$ If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)=m+3$, then, up to an ambient isometry, its image is $$S^k\\!\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{k}{m}}\\right)\n \\times\n S^{m-k}\\!\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{m-k}{m}}\\right)\n \\quad\\text{for some }1\\le k<m.$$ In particular, such a hypersurface of volume less than $a_m$ has index strictly greater than $m+3$.",
          "source_id": "PM-349.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-lorentz-family",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p=m+3$ and $$Q=\\overline B^{m+2}\\times[-1,1],\\qquad u=(v,b).$$ Identify opposite points of $\\partial Q$, and denote the quotient map by $q:Q\\longrightarrow\\mathbb {RP}^{p}$. There is a flat-continuous map $$\\Phi:\\mathbb {RP}^{p}\\longrightarrow\n \\mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\\mathbb Z_2)$$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The map $\\Phi$ is detecting in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:detecting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:detecting\">12</a>, has no concentration of mass, and satisfies $$\\Phi(q(0,0))=\\partial[\\Omega],\\qquad\n     \\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(0,0)))=|\\Sigma|,$$ where $\\Omega$ is the side of $\\Sigma$ opposite to $N$. For $r=\\sqrt{1-b^2}$ its masses obey $$\\label{eq:family-mass-bounds}\n     \\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(v,b)))\\le\n     \\begin{cases}\n      r^m|\\Sigma|,& |v|<1,\\\\\n      r^m\\sigma_m,& |v|=1.\n     \\end{cases}$$ In particular $|\\Sigma|\\ge\\gamma_m>\\sigma_m$, where $\\gamma_m$ is the constant in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:detection-gap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:detection-gap\">14</a>.*\n\n\n2.  *The central parameter is the unique maximum, in the uniform sense that for every neighborhood $U$ of $(0,0)$ in $Q$, $$\\label{eq:family-away-gap}\n     \\sup_{u\\in Q\\setminus U}\\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(u)))<|\\Sigma|.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Near $(0,0)$ these cycles are precisely the multiplicity-one boundaries of a smooth family of domains, with embedded boundaries parametrized by $\\Sigma$. The normal derivative at the center, in the parameter direction $(d,t)\\in\\mathbb R^{m+2}\\times\\mathbb R$, is $$\\label{eq:family-normal-variations}\n     w_{d,t}(x)=t+\\langle d,x\\rangle.$$ These variations form a $p$-dimensional subspace on which the area second variation is negative definite.*\n\n\n4.  *If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)>p$, there is a flat homotopy of $\\Phi$, supported in the image of a neighborhood of $(0,0)$, to a detecting map $\\widetilde\\Phi$ with no concentration of mass and $$\\label{eq:family-strict-deformation}\n     \\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb {RP}^{p}}\\mathbf M(\\widetilde\\Phi(\\xi))<|\\Sigma|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-lorentz-family",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p=m+3$ and $$Q=\\overline B^{m+2}\\times[-1,1],\\qquad u=(v,b).$$ Identify opposite points of $\\partial Q$, and denote the quotient map by $q:Q\\longrightarrow\\mathbb {RP}^{p}$. There is a flat-continuous map $$\\Phi:\\mathbb {RP}^{p}\\longrightarrow\n \\mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\\mathbb Z_2)$$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The map $\\Phi$ is detecting in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:detecting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:detecting\">12</a>, has no concentration of mass, and satisfies $$\\Phi(q(0,0))=\\partial[\\Omega],\\qquad\n     \\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(0,0)))=|\\Sigma|,$$ where $\\Omega$ is the side of $\\Sigma$ opposite to $N$. For $r=\\sqrt{1-b^2}$ its masses obey $$\\label{eq:family-mass-bounds}\n     \\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(v,b)))\\le\n     \\begin{cases}\n      r^m|\\Sigma|,& |v|<1,\\\\\n      r^m\\sigma_m,& |v|=1.\n     \\end{cases}$$ In particular $|\\Sigma|\\ge\\gamma_m>\\sigma_m$, where $\\gamma_m$ is the constant in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:detection-gap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:detection-gap\">14</a>.*\n\n\n2.  *The central parameter is the unique maximum, in the uniform sense that for every neighborhood $U$ of $(0,0)$ in $Q$, $$\\label{eq:family-away-gap}\n     \\sup_{u\\in Q\\setminus U}\\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(u)))<|\\Sigma|.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Near $(0,0)$ these cycles are precisely the multiplicity-one boundaries of a smooth family of domains, with embedded boundaries parametrized by $\\Sigma$. The normal derivative at the center, in the parameter direction $(d,t)\\in\\mathbb R^{m+2}\\times\\mathbb R$, is $$\\label{eq:family-normal-variations}\n     w_{d,t}(x)=t+\\langle d,x\\rangle.$$ These variations form a $p$-dimensional subspace on which the area second variation is negative definite.*\n\n\n4.  *If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)>p$, there is a flat homotopy of $\\Phi$, supported in the image of a neighborhood of $(0,0)$, to a detecting map $\\widetilde\\Phi$ with no concentration of mass and $$\\label{eq:family-strict-deformation}\n     \\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb {RP}^{p}}\\mathbf M(\\widetilde\\Phi(\\xi))<|\\Sigma|.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-349.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $p=m+3$ and $$Q=\\overline B^{m+2}\\times[-1,1],\\qquad u=(v,b).$$ Identify opposite points of $\\partial Q$, and denote the quotient map by $q:Q\\longrightarrow\\mathbb {RP}^{p}$. There is a flat-continuous map $$\\Phi:\\mathbb {RP}^{p}\\longrightarrow\n \\mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\\mathbb Z_2)$$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The map $\\Phi$ is detecting in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:detecting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:detecting\">12</a>, has no concentration of mass, and satisfies $$\\Phi(q(0,0))=\\partial[\\Omega],\\qquad\n     \\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(0,0)))=|\\Sigma|,$$ where $\\Omega$ is the side of $\\Sigma$ opposite to $N$. For $r=\\sqrt{1-b^2}$ its masses obey $$\\label{eq:family-mass-bounds}\n     \\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(v,b)))\\le\n     \\begin{cases}\n      r^m|\\Sigma|,& |v|<1,\\\\\n      r^m\\sigma_m,& |v|=1.\n     \\end{cases}$$ In particular $|\\Sigma|\\ge\\gamma_m>\\sigma_m$, where $\\gamma_m$ is the constant in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:detection-gap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:detection-gap\">14</a>.*\n\n\n2.  *The central parameter is the unique maximum, in the uniform sense that for every neighborhood $U$ of $(0,0)$ in $Q$, $$\\label{eq:family-away-gap}\n     \\sup_{u\\in Q\\setminus U}\\mathbf M(\\Phi(q(u)))<|\\Sigma|.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Near $(0,0)$ these cycles are precisely the multiplicity-one boundaries of a smooth family of domains, with embedded boundaries parametrized by $\\Sigma$. The normal derivative at the center, in the parameter direction $(d,t)\\in\\mathbb R^{m+2}\\times\\mathbb R$, is $$\\label{eq:family-normal-variations}\n     w_{d,t}(x)=t+\\langle d,x\\rangle.$$ These variations form a $p$-dimensional subspace on which the area second variation is negative definite.*\n\n\n4.  *If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ind}}(\\Sigma)>p$, there is a flat homotopy of $\\Phi$, supported in the image of a neighborhood of $(0,0)$, to a detecting map $\\widetilde\\Phi$ with no concentration of mass and $$\\label{eq:family-strict-deformation}\n     \\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb {RP}^{p}}\\mathbf M(\\widetilde\\Phi(\\xi))<|\\Sigma|.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-family-cone-contact",
        "exact_quote": "There is $K<1$ such that $$\\label{eq:family-spacelike-bound}\n |\\varphi(z)|\\le K|z|\\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^{m+2}).$$ For $z\\ne0$, put $h=\\varphi(z)$. Every closest point of $\\mathcal C$ to $z$ has the form $r_zx$, with $x\\in\\Sigma$ and $$\\label{eq:family-contact}\n r_z=\\sqrt{|z|^2-h^2}>0,\\qquad\n z=r_zx+hN(x),\\qquad r_z I-hA(x)\\ge0.$$ The final inequality is an inequality of self-adjoint endomorphisms of $T_x\\Sigma$.",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"

      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Every $E_{v,b}$, for $(v,b)\\in Q$, has finite perimeter.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-family-central-tube",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small $(v,b)$, the entire level $\\Lambda_{v,b}$ is $Y_{v,b}(\\Sigma)$, with $Y_{v,b}$ a smooth embedding depending smoothly on the parameters. Every level point has a unique closest-point contact in the cone representation. Moreover $$\\label{eq:family-regular-level}\n \\nabla_{S^{m+1}}\\psi_v=N'-bY_{v,b},\\qquad\n |\\nabla_{S^{m+1}}\\psi_v|=r>0\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\Lambda_{v,b}.$$ Thus $\\Lambda_{v,b}=\\partial E_{v,b}=\\partial^*E_{v,b}$ and its boundary cycle has multiplicity one.",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-349.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-operation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\geq1$ and let $V$ be a stationary integral $d$-varifold in the unit sphere $S^{d+1}$. Its Euclidean cone $\\mathcal C(V)$ is a stationary integral $(d+1)$-varifold in $\\mathbb R^{d+2}$, and $$\\label{eq:cones-mass}\n \\|\\mathcal C(V)\\|(B_R(0))\n   =\\frac{R^{d+1}}{d+1}\\mathbf M(V),\n \\qquad\n \\Theta(\\mathcal C(V),0)=\\frac{\\mathbf M(V)}{\\sigma_d}.$$ If $V$ is a finite sum of smooth closed embedded minimal hypersurfaces with positive integer multiplicities, then $\\mathcal C(V)$ is cyclic modulo two.",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-operation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\geq1$ and let $V$ be a stationary integral $d$-varifold in the unit sphere $S^{d+1}$.           "source_id": "PM-349.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\geq1$ and let $V$ be a stationary integral $d$-varifold in the unit sphere $S^{d+1}$.       "explanation": "The Euclidean cone over a stationary integral spherical varifold is stationary and integral, with explicit ball masses and vertex density, and is cyclic modulo two for smooth multiplicity-weighted hypersurface sums.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cones-density",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a stationary integral $k$-varifold in an open set $U\\subset\\mathbb R^n$. At every $z\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\|V\\|$, $\\Theta(V,z)\\geq1$. If $V=\\mathcal C$ is a cone in all of $\\mathbb R^n$, then $$\\label{eq:cones-density-comparison}\n 1\\leq\\Theta(\\mathcal C,z)\\leq\\Theta(\\mathcal C,0)\n \\qquad (z\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\|\\mathcal C\\|).$$ Every tangent cone at such a point is nonzero and has density $\\Theta(\\mathcal C,z)$ at its vertex.",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cones-density",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a stationary integral $k$-varifold in an open set $U\\subset\\mathbb R^n$. At every $z\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\|V\\|$, $\\Theta(V,z)\\geq1$.           "source_id": "PM-349.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be a stationary integral $k$-varifold in an open set $U\\subset\\mathbb R^n$. At every $z\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\|V\\|$, $\\Theta(V,z)\\geq1$.       "explanation": "Stationary integral varifolds have density at least one at every support point; for cones the vertex density bounds the density elsewhere, and tangent cones inherit the density.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cones-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\geq2$ and let $\\mathcal C$ be a stationary integral $k$-dimensional cone in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$. For $0\\ne z\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\|\\mathcal C\\|$, every tangent cone $T$ at $z$ has the form $$T=\\mathbf v(\\mathbb Re)\\times W,\\qquad e=z/|z|,$$ where $W$ is a nonzero stationary integral $(k-1)$-dimensional cone in $e^\\perp\\cong\\mathbb R^k$ and $$\\label{eq:cones-factor-density}\n \\Theta(W,0)=\\Theta(T,0)=\\Theta(\\mathcal C,z).$$ If $\\mathcal C$ is cyclic modulo two, so are $T$ and $W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-349.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cones-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\geq2$ and let $\\mathcal C$ be a stationary integral $k$-dimensional cone in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$.           "source_id": "PM-349.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $k\\geq2$ and let $\\mathcal C$ be a stationary integral $k$-dimensional cone in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Clifford volume threshold a_m** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For each integer $m\ge2$, set $$a_m=\min_{1\le k<m}\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_m(\mathcal C_{k,m-k}).$$
- **Clifford ratio** ([source](#stmt-lem-clifford-ratios)): The numbers $\vartheta_m=a_m/\sigma_m$ strictly decrease for integers $m\ge2$.
- **Sphere and ball volumes** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): We write $\sigma_j=\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_j(S^j)$ and $\omega_j=\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_j(B^j_1)$, so $\sigma_{j-1}=j\omega_j$.
- **Closed** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): The word closed means compact without boundary.
- **Morse index** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): The Morse index counts the positive eigenvalues, with multiplicity, of $$L=\Delta+m+P;$$ equivalently it is the dimension of a maximal negative subspace for the area second variation $-\int_\Sigma uLu$.
- **Density of a varifold** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): $$ Thus, with $k>0$ fixed, the derivative in $l$ of $\log(d_kd_l/d_{k+l})$ is negative. Increase the second entry of a minimizing pair for $m$ by one to obtain a strictly smaller competitor for $\vartheta_{m+1}$. This proves the strict decrease. The stated values follow from $k=l=1$ and $k=l=2$, respectively. ◻ </details> For a $d$-dimensional Euclidean varifold $V$, its density at $z$, when it exists, is $$\Theta(V,z)=\lim_{\rho\downarrow0} \frac{\\|V\\|(B_\rho(z))}{\omega_d\rho^d}.$$
- **Detecting map** ([source](#stmt-def-detecting)): A flat-continuous map $\Phi:X\to\mathcal Z_m^0(S^{m+1};\mathbb Z_2)$ from a finite CW complex is *detecting* if $$(\Phi^*\lambda)^p\ne0\quad\hbox{in }H^p(X;\mathbb Z_2).$$
- **Cyclic modulo two** ([source](#sec:cones)): We call $V$ *cyclic modulo two* if $\partial[V]=0$ locally in the ambient open set.

| q-volume-threshold | Volume of the domain under the pulled-back round metric | ≥ | $a_m$ | Integer m at least two. |

| q-clifford-ratio-dim4 | Clifford ratio in dimension four | ≤ | $\frac32$ | Dimension four. |
| q-clifford-ratio-high | Clifford ratio in high dimension | ≤ | $3/2$ | Integers m at least four. |
| q-clifford-below-twice-equator | Clifford threshold | < | $2\sigma_m$ | Integers m at least two. |
| q-multiplicity-volume | Domain volume of an immersion with a q-fold image point | ≥ | $q\sigma_m$ | Smooth minimal immersions, m at least two. |
| q-average-curvature | Integral of the squared second fundamental form | ≥ | $m\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_m(\Sigma)$ | Dimension at least two. |
| q-index-lower-bound | Morse index of the hypersurface | ≥ | $m+3$ | Dimension at least two. |
| q-detection-gap | Detection gap constant | > | $\sigma_m$ | Dimension m; defined as an infimum. |
| q-minmax-mass-window | Mass of the min-max stationary varifold | ≤ | $D_0$ | Dimension at least two. |
| q-parameter-dimension | Dimension of the projective parameter space | = | $m+3$ | Dimension at least two. |
| q-spacelike-constant | Signed cone distance | ≤ | $K\|z\|$ | All points z. |
| q-restricted-determinant | Restricted determinant of the contact matrix | ≤ | $r^m$ | Finite boosts and interior levels. |
| q-cone-vertex-density | Vertex density of the Euclidean cone | = | $\frac{\mathbf M(V)}{\sigma_d}$ | Dimension d at least one. |
| q-density-comparison | Density of a cone at a support point | ≤ | $\Theta(\mathcal C,0)$ | Support points of the cone. |
| q-cone-factor-density | Density of the split factor | = | $\Theta(\mathcal C,z)$ | Nonzero support points, k at least two. |
| q-low-density-threshold | Vertex density of the cone in the low-density proposition | < | $\vartheta_m$ | Volume theorem assumed in lower dimensions. |
| q-spherical-output-mass | Mass of the stationary spherical varifold | < | $a_m$ | Outer induction hypothesis in force. |
| q-least-counterexample-window | Least counterexample area | < | $a_m$ | Volume theorem assumed in lower dimensions. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-ratios** [claim](#stmt-lem-clifford-ratios) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clifford-ratios): Convexity of the logarithm of an interpolating volume function gives monotone Clifford ratios. -> strict decrease of the ratio and the stated bounds
- **s-immersion** [claim](#stmt-prop-immersion-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-immersion-reduction): Cone over the immersion is stationary; monotonicity at a point with several sheets bounds the vertex density from below. -> volume at least q equator volumes; reduction to embeddings
- **s-upper-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-upper-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-upper-tests): Distinct-point and diagonal contact calculations with Hessian traces and reflection. -> logarithmic differential inequality for upper tests
- **s-average-curvature** [claim](#stmt-thm-curvature-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-curvature-average): Contradiction using a Poisson potential and the upper test inequality. -> integral curvature lower bound
- **s-normal-independence** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-normal-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-normal-independence): Obata sphere rigidity forces total geodesy, a contradiction. -> injectivity of the normal coordinate map
- **s-conformal** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-conformal-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-conformal-tests): Degree argument for the balancing map and a derivative formula for conformal factor integrals. -> balanced conformal map and strict decrease of integrals
- **s-index-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-prop-index-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-index-rigidity): Spectral comparison with balanced test functions forces equality, parallel second fundamental form and a product classification. -> index at least m+3 with Clifford rigidity
- **s-filling-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-minmax-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-minmax-cover): Small fillings and uniqueness up to complement give a covering chart. -> two-sheeted covering with complement deck map
- **s-projective** [claim](#stmt-lem-minmax-projective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-minmax-projective): Radial projection to a ball and the cohomology ring of projective space. -> detecting map on projective space
- **s-detection-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-detection-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detection-gap): Constrained isoperimetric minimization and an odd-map vanishing argument. -> mass threshold above the equator volume
- **s-cubical** [claim](#stmt-lem-minmax-cubical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-minmax-cubical): Embed projective space in symmetric matrices and use a retraction from a cubical neighborhood. -> detecting map on a cubical complex
- **s-minmax** [claim](#stmt-thm-minmax) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-minmax): Discretization, comparison of widths with the detection gap, and the Almgren-Pitts existence and regularity theorems. -> stationary integral varifold with mass in the stated window
- **s-cone-contact** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-cone-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-cone-contact): Height maximum over the hypersurface and its second-order condition. -> spacelike bound and contact description
- **s-family-graph** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-graph): Monotonicity of a one-variable function from the Lipschitz property of distance. -> global graph and compactified extension
- **s-all-levels** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-all-levels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-all-levels): Area formula on the source with an arithmetic-geometric mean bound on the contact matrix. -> restricted perimeter bound at every level
- **s-no-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-no-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-no-concentration): Sequential argument with dominated convergence on the fixed source, including infinite boosts. -> uniform absence of concentration
- **s-strict-loss** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-strict-loss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-strict-loss): Contradiction using a fixed source patch where the truncated determinant is strictly smaller. -> uniform area loss away from the center
- **s-central-tube** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-central-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-central-tube): Tubular neighborhood of the smooth cone gives unique contacts near the center. -> smooth embedded central level of multiplicity one
- **s-lorentz-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-lorentz-family) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Assemble detection, no concentration and strict loss; remove the central maximum with an extra negative direction through a Morse estimate. -> detecting family with mass strictly below the area when index is large
- **s-cone-operation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cone-operation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cone-operation): Polar decomposition of the first variation and radial cutoff. -> stationary integral cone with explicit mass and cyclicity
- **s-cones-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-cones-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cones-density): Monotonicity at nearby rectifiable points and ball inclusions. -> density at least one and cone density comparison
- **s-cones-splitting** [claim](#stmt-lem-cones-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cones-splitting): Translation invariance along the radial direction, coarea slicing and stationarity testing. -> tangent cone is a line times a lower-dimensional cone
- **s-cones-allard** [claim](#stmt-lem-cones-allard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cones-allard): Apply Allard interior regularity on small rescaled balls, then higher regularity. -> smooth multiplicity-one regularity near the point
- **s-low-density-cones** [claim](#stmt-prop-low-density-cones) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-low-density-cones): Induction on cone dimension using splitting, Allard regularity and the outer volume induction. -> low-density cones are planes; smooth minimal link in the top dimension
- **s-spherical-output** [claim](#stmt-cor-cones-spherical-output) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cones-spherical-output): Apply the cone proposition to the cone over the varifold and read off the link. -> smooth connected embedded multiplicity-one hypersurface
- **s-attainment** [claim](#stmt-prop-attainment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-attainment): Compactness of cones over a minimizing sequence with exact mass identification. -> attained least counterexample with area window
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-attainment-2): Induction on dimension: a least counterexample has large index, so the deformed family and min-max produce a smaller counterexample. -> the volume theorem in all dimensions

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-clifford-ratio-decreasing**: The ratio of the smallest Clifford-product volume to the equator volume strictly decreases with the dimension; this finite quantity can be recomputed for any given dimension.
- **inv-ratio-dimension-two**: In dimension two the ratio equals pi over two, which is below two.
- **inv-ratio-dimension-four-bound**: From dimension four on the ratio is at most three halves.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-as-proof**: Treat the numerical agreement of the ratio code for a handful of dimensions as evidence that the volume theorem holds for a given immersion. -> The code only evaluates the Clifford threshold; it says nothing about arbitrary minimal immersions, whose bound rests on index rigidity, min-max and cone induction.
- **anti-finite-check-all-dimensions**: Conclude strict decrease of the ratio in every dimension from checking finitely many dimensions. -> A finite scan cannot replace the analytic convexity argument that covers all integers.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The main results (min-max existence, index rigidity, cone induction) are non-constructive and are not tested. Only the finite explicit quantity in the Clifford ratio lemma is computed: the minimum over the splitting index of the Clifford product volume divided by the equator volume.

```python
# INPUTS: one positive integer m >= 2 on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: m is an integer with m >= 2.
# OUTPUTS: the ratio of the smallest Clifford-product volume to the equator volume, printed with nine decimals.
# TIME: O(m) evaluations of the volume formula.
# SPACE: O(1) beyond the generator.
# ARITHMETIC: IEEE double precision with logarithms and log-gamma; absolute error far below 1e-8.
# FAILURE: malformed or missing input raises an exception and gives a nonzero exit.
import math
import sys


def log_sigma(j):
    return math.log(2.0) + (j + 1) / 2 * math.log(math.pi) - math.lgamma((j + 1) / 2)


def log_vol(k, l):
    m = k + l
    return log_sigma(k) + log_sigma(l) + k / 2 * math.log(k / m) + l / 2 * math.log(l / m)


m = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
best = min(log_vol(k, m - k) for k in range(1, m))
print("%.9f" % math.exp(best - log_sigma(m)))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence and lower-bound theorem through min-max and cone induction. It gives no executable procedure that could be packaged as a library. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamics, time stepping or interactive model appears. The setting is static minimal hypersurface geometry in a round sphere. |
| tools | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The sharp threshold can serve as a sanity bound when auditing numerical area-minimization or mean curvature flow output on spheres. The paper itself provides no tool, discretization or error control. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is non-constructive: infimum attainment by compactness and min-max existence give no finite procedure with stated cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is defined. Cubical complexes appear only as topological parameter spaces for min-max. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the results concerns parallel computation, arithmetic units or memory layouts. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security notion occurs; the content is purely geometric. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning model, data or loss function is involved. Detection is a cohomological condition, not a statistical one. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The multi-parameter sweepout on projective space, with a local index deformation lowering the maximum, is a template for designing test families in saddle-point search. No discretization or convergence guarantee is given. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, replication or concurrency structure appears. |
| other | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Within pure geometry the result is a reusable benchmark: a sharp volume threshold, index rigidity and a cone-density regularity scheme that other minimal-submanifold problems can adapt. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-not-totally-geodesic** (hypothesis): The volume bound is asserted only when the image of the immersion is not totally geodesic. For totally geodesic images the equator has smaller volume than the Clifford threshold, so the bound fails there. Blocked use: ['Applying the lower bound to the equator or any great sphere', 'Using the threshold as a bound for a surface mesh before checking it is not totally geodesic']
- **lim-closed-domain** (hypothesis): All domains and hypersurfaces are closed, meaning compact without boundary. The integral identities, the filling cover and the cone arguments rely on this. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to minimal surfaces with boundary or to Plateau-type problems', 'Applying the bound to complete non-compact minimal hypersurfaces']
- **lim-codim-one-sphere** (hypothesis): The argument uses two-sidedness, fullness and region complements of a hypersurface in the unit round sphere. Higher-codimension submanifolds and other ambient manifolds are not covered. Blocked use: ['Using the volume threshold for minimal submanifolds of codimension greater than one', 'Using the threshold for minimal hypersurfaces in non-round or non-spherical ambient spaces']
- **lim-minmax-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The min-max output and the gap constant come from compactness and existence theorems. They give no finite procedure, discretization or error bound. Blocked use: ['Reading the min-max step as a numerical solver with a convergence guarantee', 'Computing the detection gap constant by a stated algorithm']
- **lim-gap-constant-dimension** (approximation): The detection gap constant is only shown to exist and to exceed the equator volume. It depends on the dimension and no explicit value is supplied. Blocked use: ['Using a specific numerical value of the gap in a computation', 'Treating the gap as an explicit universal constant']
- **lim-regularity-range** (edge_case): Smooth embedded regularity of the min-max output is stated only for a bounded range of low dimensions. Beyond it the paper uses only stationarity and integrality, and regularity must come from the cone argument. Blocked use: ['Assuming a smooth embedded min-max varifold in high dimensions without the cone induction']
- **lim-induction-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The low-density cone classification and the least-counterexample statement presuppose the volume theorem in all lower dimensions. They are conditional results inside the induction, not standalone theorems. Blocked use: ['Citing the cone classification as unconditional in a given dimension', 'Using the least-counterexample statement without establishing the lower-dimensional cases']
- **lim-cyclic-low-dimension** (edge_case): In the first two dimensions where the cone result is invoked, an additional cyclicity modulo two condition is required. Without it the density threshold argument for one-dimensional cones is not available. Blocked use: ['Dropping cyclicity when applying the low-density cone proposition in the lowest dimensions']
- **lim-index-deformation** (hypothesis): Lowering the maximum of the Lorentz family requires the Morse index to exceed the parameter dimension. At equality the deformation is unavailable, which is why index rigidity is needed. Blocked use: ['Applying the strict-deformation conclusion to a hypersurface whose index equals the parameter dimension']
- **lim-ratio-code-scope** (formal_scope): The finite ratio computation covers only the explicit Clifford ratio in a limited range of integer dimensions. It does not test or certify the monotonicity for every integer or any step of the volume proof. Blocked use: ['Presenting numerical agreement in a few dimensions as proof of strict decrease for all dimensions', 'Using the ratio code as evidence for the volume theorem for a particular immersion']
- **lim-ratio-precision** (approximation): The reference ratio computation uses floating point with logarithms of the gamma function, so reported values are approximations of the exact quantities and large dimensions are only checked against tolerances. Blocked use: ['Using the printed decimals as exact values', 'Using floating-point output to decide a strict inequality that is tight']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Solomon–Yau least-volume conjecture for minimal hypersurfaces of unit round spheres: in every dimension <i>m</i> ≥ 2, a closed connected minimal immersion with non-totally-geodesic image has volume at least the smallest <i>m</i>-dimensional minimal Clifford product. Volume is measured on the domain, so covering multiplicities are included.
